10.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/voice must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

10.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 11: SKILLS
================================================================================

The Skills page is an administrative surface for reusable skills,
schemas, prompts, tools, dependencies, versions, publication status,
permissions, testing, and lifecycle management. It must operate within
the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must
never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/skills. The server must
require the dedicated permission for Skills before returning page data.
The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint used by
the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

11.001 Skills requirement 1: Allow rollback only after compatibility
checks against the current environment and dependency graph. For this
page, apply that rule specifically to reusable skills, schemas, prompts,
tools, dependencies, versions, publication status, permissions, testing,
and lifecycle management, using the selected skills context where
applicable. 11.002 Skills requirement 2: Keep audit records
append-oriented and prevent ordinary administrators from editing
historical events. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
reusable skills, schemas, prompts, tools, dependencies, versions,
publication status, permissions, testing, and lifecycle management,
using the selected skills context where applicable. 11.003 Skills
requirement 3: Ensure deletion flows identify dependent resources and

applicable. 11.004 Skills requirement 4: Use soft deletion where

11.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/skills must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

11.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 12: TEACH THE AI
================================================================================

The Teach the AI page is an administrative surface for global
instructions, knowledge sources, behavior rules, retrieval sources,
evaluation sets, versioning, approvals, and rollback. It must operate
within the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and
must never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/teach-the-ai. The
server must require the dedicated permission for Teach the AI before
returning page data. The permission check must be repeated by every
mutation endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for
UX but is never sufficient for authorization.

12.001 Teach the AI requirement 1: Use soft deletion where appropriate
for recoverable administrative resources, with explicit purge controls
for authorized roles. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
global instructions, knowledge sources, behavior rules, retrieval
sources, evaluation sets, versioning, approvals, and rollback, using the
selected teach the ai context where applicable. 12.002 Teach the AI
requirement 2: Enforce rate limits on expensive administrative endpoints
and provide a retry-after signal when throttled. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to global instructions, knowledge sources,
behavior rules, retrieval sources, evaluation sets, versioning,
approvals, and rollback, using the selected teach the ai context where
applicable. 12.003 Teach the AI requirement 3: Record provider request
identifiers when returned so operational incidents can be traced without

12.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/teach-the-ai must return only fields permitted by the
caller’s administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose
pagination metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request
against the latest resource version and must return an operation
identifier when work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s
standard error envelope and must not expose provider credentials or
protected implementation details.

12.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 13: INTEGRATIONS
================================================================================

The Integrations page is an administrative surface for OAuth apps, MCP
servers, API connectors, webhooks, provider credentials, scopes, health
checks, permissions, and lifecycle. It must operate within the selected
tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never become a
normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/integrations. The
server must require the dedicated permission for Integrations before
returning page data. The permission check must be repeated by every
mutation endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for
UX but is never sufficient for authorization.

13.001 Integrations requirement 1: Test authorization both through
normal navigation and direct API invocation; both paths must produce the
same security decision. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
OAuth apps, MCP servers, API connectors, webhooks, provider credentials,
scopes, health checks, permissions, and lifecycle, using the selected
integrations context where applicable. 13.002 Integrations requirement
2: Make the page resilient to provider outages by retaining
configuration visibility while clearly disabling unavailable runtime
actions. For this page, apply that rule specifically to OAuth apps, MCP
servers, API connectors, webhooks, provider credentials, scopes, health
checks, permissions, and lifecycle, using the selected integrations
context where applicable. 13.003 Integrations requirement 3:
Differentiate configuration errors from provider errors, network errors,
authorization errors, and internal failures. For this page, apply that
rule specifically to OAuth apps, MCP servers, API connectors, webhooks,
provider credentials, scopes, health checks, permissions, and lifecycle,
using the selected integrations context where applicable. 13.004
Integrations requirement 4: Support safe concurrency by detecting
competing edits and avoiding silent last-write-wins for critical
settings. For this page, apply that rule specifically to OAuth apps, MCP
servers, API connectors, webhooks, provider credentials, scopes, health
checks, permissions, and lifecycle, using the selected integrations
context where applicable. 13.005 Integrations requirement 5: Provide
contextual warnings before changing values that affect many users,

13.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/integrations must return only fields permitted by the
caller’s administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose
pagination metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request
against the latest resource version and must return an operation
identifier when work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s
standard error envelope and must not expose provider credentials or
protected implementation details.

13.DATA DATA MODEL

13.SEC SECURITY MODEL

13.UX STATES

13.AUDIT EVENTS

13.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 14: CUSTOM CODE
================================================================================

The Custom Code page is an administrative surface for server functions,
scripts, packages, runtimes, builds, environment variables, deployment
policies, logs, and code governance. It must operate within the selected
tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never become a
normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/custom-code. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Custom Code before returning
page data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation
endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but
is never sufficient for authorization.

14.001 Custom Code requirement 1: Support safe concurrency by detecting
competing edits and avoiding silent last-write-wins for critical
settings. For this page, apply that rule specifically to server
functions, scripts, packages, runtimes, builds, environment variables,
deployment policies, logs, and code governance, using the selected
custom code context where applicable. 14.002 Custom Code requirement 2:
Provide contextual warnings before changing values that affect many
users, projects, agents, or deployments. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to server functions, scripts, packages, runtimes, builds,
environment variables, deployment policies, logs, and code governance,
using the selected custom code context where applicable. 14.003 Custom
Code requirement 3: Show affected-scope estimates only when calculated
from authoritative data and label estimates as estimates. For this page,

14.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/custom-code must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

14.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 15: GITHUB SYNC
================================================================================

The GitHub Sync page is an administrative surface for repositories,
branches, credentials, sync direction, commits, pull requests,
deployments, conflict handling, and synchronization policy. It must
operate within the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role
scope and must never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/github-sync. The server
must require the dedicated permission for GitHub Sync before returning
page data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation
endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but
is never sufficient for authorization.

15.001 GitHub Sync requirement 1: Keep administrative page telemetry
free of secret values, authentication headers, raw tokens, and
unnecessary personal data. For this page, apply that rule specifically
to repositories, branches, credentials, sync direction, commits, pull
requests, deployments, conflict handling, and synchronization policy,
using the selected github sync context where applicable. 15.002 GitHub
Sync requirement 2: Include automated tests for normal, unauthorized,

15.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/github-sync must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

15.DATA DATA MODEL

15.SEC SECURITY MODEL

15.UX STATES

15.AUDIT EVENTS

15.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 16: MONEY
================================================================================

The Money page is an administrative surface for revenue metrics, credit
economics, provider costs, transaction events, refunds, financial
reporting, and operational controls. It must operate within the selected
tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never become a
normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/money. The server must
require the dedicated permission for Money before returning page data.
The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint used by
the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

16.001 Money requirement 1: Define the page’s primary purpose and
identify which administrative resource is being managed. For this page,
apply that rule specifically to revenue metrics, credit economics,
provider costs, transaction events, refunds, financial reporting, and
operational controls, using the selected money context where applicable.
16.002 Money requirement 2: Provide a compact summary strip showing the
most important live state before detailed controls. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to revenue metrics, credit economics, provider
costs, transaction events, refunds, financial reporting, and operational
controls, using the selected money context where applicable. 16.003
Money requirement 3: Separate read-only telemetry from mutable

where applicable. 16.004 Money requirement 4: Implement server-side

16.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/money must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

16.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 17: BILLING
================================================================================

The Billing page is an administrative surface for plans, prices, trials,
subscriptions, invoices, payment providers, entitlements, proration,
taxes, cancellations, and billing support. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/billing. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Billing before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

17.001 Billing requirement 1: Implement server-side pagination for
potentially large collections and preserve cursor state during
navigation. For this page, apply that rule specifically to plans,
prices, trials, subscriptions, invoices, payment providers,
entitlements, proration, taxes, cancellations, and billing support,
using the selected billing context where applicable. 17.002 Billing
requirement 2: Support scoped search using normalized names,
identifiers, status values, and relevant metadata. For this page, apply

invoices, payment providers, entitlements, proration, taxes,
cancellations, and billing support, using the selected billing context
where applicable. 17.003 Billing requirement 3: Allow filtering by

Separate read-only telemetry from mutable configuration so users can
understand consequences before editing. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to plans, prices, trials, subscriptions, invoices, payment
providers, entitlements, proration, taxes, cancellations, and billing
support, using the selected billing context where applicable. 17.148
Billing requirement 148: Implement server-side pagination for
potentially large collections and preserve cursor state during
navigation. For this page, apply that rule specifically to plans,
prices, trials, subscriptions, invoices, payment providers,
entitlements, proration, taxes, cancellations, and billing support,
using the selected billing context where applicable. 17.149 Billing
requirement 149: Support scoped search using normalized names,
identifiers, status values, and relevant metadata. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to plans, prices, trials, subscriptions,
invoices, payment providers, entitlements, proration, taxes,
cancellations, and billing support, using the selected billing context
where applicable. 17.150 Billing requirement 150: Allow filtering by
environment and tenant without changing the authorization context
implicitly. For this page, apply that rule specifically to plans,
prices, trials, subscriptions, invoices, payment providers,
entitlements, proration, taxes, cancellations, and billing support,
using the selected billing context where applicable.

17.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/billing must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

17.SEC SECURITY MODEL

17.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 18: VISITOR ANALYTICS
================================================================================

The Visitor Analytics page is an administrative surface for traffic,
sessions, acquisition, events, funnels, retention, privacy controls,
aggregation, dashboards, and export rules. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/visitor-analytics. The
server must require the dedicated permission for Visitor Analytics
before returning page data. The permission check must be repeated by
every mutation endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is
useful for UX but is never sufficient for authorization.

18.001 Visitor Analytics requirement 1: Provide a clear empty state
explaining what creates the first resource without pretending that
sample data is live. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
traffic, sessions, acquisition, events, funnels, retention, privacy
controls, aggregation, dashboards, and export rules, using the selected
visitor analytics context where applicable. 18.002 Visitor Analytics
requirement 2: Expose provider-specific fields only after capability
discovery confirms that the provider supports them. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to traffic, sessions, acquisition, events,
funnels, retention, privacy controls, aggregation, dashboards, and
export rules, using the selected visitor analytics context where
applicable. 18.003 Visitor Analytics requirement 3: Use optimistic UI
only for reversible presentation changes; authoritative backend state

controls, aggregation, dashboards, and export rules, using the selected
visitor analytics context where applicable. 18.004 Visitor Analytics

applicable. 18.005 Visitor Analytics requirement 5: Make asynchronous

18.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/visitor-analytics must return only fields permitted by the
caller’s administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose
pagination metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request
against the latest resource version and must return an operation
identifier when work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s
standard error envelope and must not expose provider credentials or
protected implementation details.

18.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 19: EMBED
================================================================================

The Embed page is an administrative surface for embedded chat, widgets,
SDK configuration, allowed origins, appearance, behavior,
authentication, quotas, and deployment controls. It must operate within
the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must
never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/embed. The server must
require the dedicated permission for Embed before returning page data.
The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint used by
the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

19.001 Embed requirement 1: Handle stale data by detecting version
conflicts and asking the administrator to review the latest state. For
this page, apply that rule specifically to embedded chat, widgets, SDK
configuration, allowed origins, appearance, behavior, authentication,
quotas, and deployment controls, using the selected embed context where
applicable. 19.002 Embed requirement 2: Make asynchronous work resumable
where supported and expose progress without exposing internal

records. For this page, apply that rule specifically to embedded chat,
widgets, SDK configuration, allowed origins, appearance, behavior,
authentication, quotas, and deployment controls, using the selected
embed context where applicable.

19.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/embed must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

19.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 20: MARKETPLACE
================================================================================

The Marketplace page is an administrative surface for extensions,
agents, skills, templates, submissions, reviews, publishing, versioning,
seller controls, moderation, and distribution. It must operate within
the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must
never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/marketplace. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Marketplace before returning
page data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation
endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but
is never sufficient for authorization.

20.001 Marketplace requirement 1: Record actor, target, action, result,
timestamp, environment, and correlation identifier for auditable
mutations. For this page, apply that rule specifically to extensions,
agents, skills, templates, submissions, reviews, publishing, versioning,
seller controls, moderation, and distribution, using the selected
marketplace context where applicable. 20.002 Marketplace requirement 2:
Require confirmation text or an explicit secondary confirmation for
irreversible operations when appropriate. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to extensions, agents, skills, templates, submissions,
reviews, publishing, versioning, seller controls, moderation, and
distribution, using the selected marketplace context where applicable.

20.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/marketplace must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

20.SEC SECURITY MODEL

20.AUDIT EVENTS

20.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 21: DOMAIN
================================================================================

The Domain page is an administrative surface for custom domains, DNS
instructions, certificates, verification, routing, redirects,
environments, status, and domain lifecycle. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/domain. The server must
require the dedicated permission for Domain before returning page data.
The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint used by
the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

21.001 Domain requirement 1: Validate referenced resources before saving
relationships so broken dependencies cannot be silently stored. For this
page, apply that rule specifically to custom domains, DNS instructions,
certificates, verification, routing, redirects, environments, status,
and domain lifecycle, using the selected domain context where
applicable. 21.002 Domain requirement 2: Use transactional writes when
multiple records must change together; otherwise expose partial-failure

21.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/domain must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

21.DATA DATA MODEL

21.SEC SECURITY MODEL

21.UX STATES

21.AUDIT EVENTS

21.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 22: SECRETS
================================================================================

The Secrets page is an administrative surface for secret records,
encryption, rotation, versioning, environment scoping, access grants,
provider credentials, and exposure controls. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/secrets. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Secrets before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

22.001 Secrets requirement 1: Expose health diagnostics separately from
remediation actions so observing a fault cannot accidentally mutate it.
For this page, apply that rule specifically to secret records,
encryption, rotation, versioning, environment scoping, access grants,
provider credentials, and exposure controls, using the selected secrets
context where applicable. 22.002 Secrets requirement 2: Keep sensitive
values masked and provide rotation or replacement flows rather than

22.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/secrets must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

22.DATA DATA MODEL

22.SEC SECURITY MODEL

22.UX STATES

22.AUDIT EVENTS

22.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 23: SECURITY
================================================================================

The Security page is an administrative surface for authentication
policies, sessions, MFA, authorization, IP rules, threat controls,
security events, data protection, and incident response. It must operate
within the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and
must never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/security. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Security before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

23.001 Security requirement 1: Support import/export through validated
schemas with size, type, and authorization checks. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to authentication policies, sessions, MFA,
authorization, IP rules, threat controls, security events, data
protection, and incident response, using the selected security context
where applicable. 23.002 Security requirement 2: Prevent unauthorized
cross-tenant references when administrators search globally. For this

23.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/security must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

23.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 24: DISCOVERABILITY
================================================================================

The Discoverability page is an administrative surface for search
indexing, public metadata, robots policy, sitemap, previews, public
visibility, content controls, and discovery governance. It must operate
within the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and
must never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/discoverability. The
server must require the dedicated permission for Discoverability before
returning page data. The permission check must be repeated by every
mutation endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for
UX but is never sufficient for authorization.

24.001 Discoverability requirement 1: Provide keyboard-accessible menus,
dialogs, tabs, tables, filters, and confirmation controls. For this
page, apply that rule specifically to search indexing, public metadata,
robots policy, sitemap, previews, public visibility, content controls,
and discovery governance, using the selected discoverability context
where applicable. 24.002 Discoverability requirement 2: Expose
machine-readable error codes internally while presenting actionable
human-readable messages in the UI. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to search indexing, public metadata, robots policy,
sitemap, previews, public visibility, content controls, and discovery
governance, using the selected discoverability context where applicable.

24.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/discoverability must return only fields permitted by the
caller’s administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose
pagination metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request
against the latest resource version and must return an operation
identifier when work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s
standard error envelope and must not expose provider credentials or
protected implementation details.

24.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 25: ACTIVITY LOG
================================================================================

The Activity Log page is an administrative surface for immutable
administrative events, actor identity, timestamps, target resources,
before/after metadata, filtering, retention, exports, and investigation
workflows. It must operate within the selected tenant, workspace,
environment, and role scope and must never become a normal end-user
navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/activity-log. The
server must require the dedicated permission for Activity Log before
returning page data. The permission check must be repeated by every
mutation endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for
UX but is never sufficient for authorization.

25.001 Activity Log requirement 1: Cache non-sensitive metadata with
explicit invalidation rules so administrative dashboards remain fast
without becoming stale indefinitely. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to immutable administrative events, actor identity,
timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata, filtering,
retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the selected
activity log context where applicable. 25.002 Activity Log requirement
2: Use background workers for expensive scans, rebuilds, imports,

25.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/activity-log must return only fields permitted by the
caller’s administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose
pagination metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request
against the latest resource version and must return an operation
identifier when work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s
standard error envelope and must not expose provider credentials or
protected implementation details.

25.DATA DATA MODEL

25.SEC SECURITY MODEL

25.UX STATES

25.AUDIT EVENTS

25.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE CROSS-PAGE ARCHITECTURE
================================================================================

ADMIN CONSOLE SHELL

The shell owns tenant selection, environment selection, administrator
identity, global search, notifications, command navigation, support
access, and session controls. It must not load every page’s sensitive
data at once. Each route fetches the minimum authorized dataset required
for its current view.

ADMIN SEARCH

Global administrative search must be permission-aware before indexing or
returning a result. Search suggestions must not reveal resource names
from scopes the actor cannot access. Results should be ranked by exact
identifier matches, normalized names, recent activity, and resource type
while preserving authorization boundaries.

COMMAND PALETTE

The command palette may expose administrative actions only after
evaluating the current actor’s permissions and selected environment.
Commands that can mutate production configuration must display the
target and require the same confirmation policy as the underlying page
action.

SESSION SECURITY

Administrative sessions should use shorter idle windows than ordinary
user sessions when appropriate to the deployment’s security policy.
Re-authentication must be possible without losing harmless navigation
context. A revoked session must invalidate subsequent privileged API
requests rather than merely hiding the console.

STEP-UP AUTHENTICATION

The platform should classify operations by impact. Read-only operational
inspection can use the normal administrative session. Credential
rotation, domain ownership changes, destructive data operations, billing
changes, production deployment, security-policy changes, and other
high-impact actions should support step-up authentication according to
configured policy.

ENVIRONMENT SAFETY

Every page must display whether the administrator is operating against
development, staging, or production. Production mutations should have
unmistakable contextual confirmation and should never be triggered
merely because the browser restored an old environment selection.

CONFIGURATION VERSIONING

Where a setting can affect runtime behavior, store a versioned
representation. The UI should permit comparison between the active
version and a proposed version, identify the actor and timestamp, and
validate dependencies before activation.

DEPENDENCY GRAPH

Administrative resources should be represented as a dependency graph.
For example, a deployed agent may depend on a skill, integration, secret
reference, model provider, storage location, and workflow. Before
disabling or deleting a dependency, calculate affected resources and
block or warn according to policy.

BACKGROUND JOBS

Expensive administrative work must execute through durable jobs. Jobs
require a stable identifier, owner scope, requested action, state
machine, progress metadata where available, start and completion
timestamps, cancellation semantics, retry policy, and final verification
result.

QUEUE FAIRNESS

Prevent one organization or one administrator from monopolizing
administrative worker capacity. Use quotas and fair scheduling
appropriate to the deployment. Critical security operations may receive
priority without allowing ordinary workloads to starve indefinitely.

PROVIDER ABSTRACTION

Provider adapters must expose capability metadata, authentication
requirements, rate limits, supported operations, idempotency behavior,
error mapping, health status, and verification methods. The Admin
Console should consume this normalized interface rather than scattering
provider-specific logic through page components.

MCP ADMINISTRATION BOUNDARY

MCP servers can be registered, inspected, authorized, enabled, disabled,
tested, and revoked from the Admin Console. Registration does not grant
every user access. Runtime access must be resolved against organization
policy, user authorization, tool scopes, and the action’s risk
classification.

MCP TOOL DISCOVERY

Do not hard-code a ceiling of 1,000 or 10,000 applications. Maintain a
provider-neutral registry capable of lazy discovery, capability
indexing, semantic search, per-tenant visibility, connection health,
multiple accounts, and dynamic tool refresh. Only the relevant tool
schemas should enter an agent’s working context.

CREDENTIAL VAULT BOUNDARY

Store credentials in a dedicated secure mechanism. The Admin Console may
manage metadata and rotation workflows but should not routinely display
secret material. Execution services receive narrowly scoped credentials
only for the operation that needs them and must prevent those
credentials from entering model prompts, chat history, source code,
screenshots, analytics, or ordinary logs.

AUDIT ARCHITECTURE

Audit records must identify actor, organization, environment, action,
target, timestamp, outcome, correlation ID, and safe metadata describing
the change. Sensitive payloads should be omitted or cryptographically
protected according to the deployment’s audit requirements. Ordinary
administrators must not be able to rewrite historical records.

OBSERVABILITY

Use separate metrics for page latency, API latency, worker latency,
provider latency, error rate, authorization denials, queue depth, cache
effectiveness, and verification failures. Administrative dashboards
should not become a side channel for secrets or private user content.

DISASTER RECOVERY

Critical administrative configuration should be backed up according to
the platform’s recovery objectives. Restores must be tested. Recovery
procedures must preserve tenant isolation, secret references, audit
integrity, and environment boundaries.

BACKUP VERIFICATION

A backup is not considered healthy merely because a job completed.
Periodically verify that representative objects can be restored and that
checksums, metadata, relationships, and access controls remain coherent.

DATA RETENTION

Every administrative data class should have a retention policy. Activity
logs, analytics, backups, configuration history, deleted resources, and
operational traces may have different retention periods. The
implementation must not silently retain sensitive data forever.

PRIVACY

Administrative analytics must use the minimum data needed for the stated
operational purpose. Where visitor analytics or user behavior is
involved, apply the platform’s privacy controls and avoid exposing
individual-level information to administrators who do not require it.

ACCESS REVIEWS

Support periodic review of administrator memberships and permissions.
Provide an auditable view of who has access to each privileged
capability and when that access was granted or changed.

BREAK-GLASS ACCESS

If the platform supports emergency administrative access, it must be
explicitly marked, time-bounded, strongly authenticated, narrowly
scoped, and heavily audited. Emergency access must not become a hidden
permanent superuser path.

CHANGE MANAGEMENT

High-impact configuration changes should support a change record
containing intent, actor, target, proposed state, approval where
required, execution result, and rollback information. This allows
support teams to reconstruct what happened without relying on chat
transcripts.

ADMIN API CONSISTENCY

All administrative endpoints should use a common envelope for success
metadata, operation identifiers, pagination, validation errors,
authorization errors, rate-limit responses, and provider failures. The
UI should not need a separate bespoke error parser for every page.

RATE LIMITING

Apply per-actor, per-organization, and per-operation limits where
appropriate. Expensive operations should be protected independently from
inexpensive reads. Rate-limit messages should remain actionable without
revealing internal capacity details that could aid abuse.

IDEMPOTENCY

Mutations such as creating resources, rotating credentials, initiating
deployments, charging money, publishing marketplace entries, or changing
domains must use provider-supported idempotency or an equivalent
server-side deduplication strategy.

TRANSACTION BOUNDARIES

When one administrative action affects multiple records, define its
transaction boundary explicitly. If an external provider cannot
participate in a database transaction, persist a durable operation state
and reconciliation strategy instead of pretending that local and remote
state changed atomically.

RECONCILIATION

Background reconciliation should compare authoritative provider state
with KYMEX records for integrations, subscriptions, deployments,
domains, storage, and other externally managed resources. Differences
should become visible incidents or actionable drift records rather than
silent inconsistencies.

ADMIN NOTIFICATIONS

Notify authorized administrators about failed critical jobs, security
events, expiring certificates, integration failures, billing anomalies,
storage pressure, and deployment failures according to configured
notification policies. Do not include raw credentials or unnecessarily
sensitive user content.

SUPPORT DIAGNOSTICS

Provide a safe diagnostic bundle containing operation IDs, timestamps,
error classes, provider request IDs, relevant configuration versions,
and non-sensitive environment metadata. Never create a diagnostic export
that automatically dumps secrets.

MOBILE ADMINISTRATION

Mobile access must not simply shrink desktop tables. Prioritize critical
state, provide expandable details, preserve confirmation context, and
ensure dangerous actions cannot be triggered accidentally by touch.
Sensitive screens should respect device-level privacy considerations.

ACCESSIBILITY

Administrative controls must have semantic labels, visible focus,
logical tab order, keyboard-operable menus, accessible dialog behavior,
screen-reader announcements for asynchronous state, and sufficient
non-color indicators. The Blue/Green/Gray design system must never be
the only way to communicate status.

PERFORMANCE

Use route-level code splitting for the Admin Console, server-side
pagination, selective data loading, cache invalidation, debounced
search, and background workers for heavy operations. Do not preload
sensitive datasets simply to make navigation appear faster.

SECURITY TESTING

Test tenant isolation, role escalation, IDOR-style resource access,
stale-session reuse, revoked-token behavior, CSRF protections where
relevant, secret leakage, log leakage, unsafe redirects, untrusted
provider output, prompt injection through external content, and mutation
replay.

RELEASE GATES

No Admin Console release is complete until route authorization, API
authorization, audit events, high-risk confirmation, error handling,
mobile accessibility, provider failure behavior, and truthful
post-action verification pass automated and manual acceptance tests.

PART 9: KYMEX AI — UNIVERSAL COMPUTER-USE, BROWSER AUTOMATION, MCP APP
ORCHESTRATION, CREDENTIAL-AWARE ACTIONS, SCREENSHOT, AND TOOL DISCOVERY
================================================================================

IMPORTANT IMPLEMENTATION DIRECTIVE

This section is ADDITIVE ONLY.

Do NOT replace, summarize, condense, rewrite, delete, reorder, or weaken
any previous section of the KYMEX AI Prompts. Preserve all previously
specified product capabilities, pages, components, workflows, security
requirements, providers, models, agents, builders, media tools,
integrations, and Control Center functionality.

This section adds a universal action layer that turns the KYMEX AI Agent
/ Chatbot into an intelligent computer-use and application-orchestration
agent.

The goal is not merely to create a chatbot that can describe how to
perform actions. The goal is to create an agent that can, when the user
authorizes it and the connected service permits it, actually perform
supported actions, observe their results, recover from routine failures,
and report truthfully what happened.

The agent must be SMART, FAST, INTELLIGENT, EFFECTIVE, CONTEXT-AWARE,
CAPABILITY-AWARE, SECURITY-AWARE, and ACTION-ORIENTED.

The agent must not claim to have clicked, logged in, uploaded,
downloaded, created, changed, sent, published, deployed, purchased,
booked, synchronized, or otherwise completed an action unless the
underlying execution layer actually confirms that action.

  --------------------------------
  9.1 UNIVERSAL ACTION PRINCIPLE
  --------------------------------

9.1.001 The main KYMEX AI Agent must support conversational requests and
action requests through the same intelligent interface.

9.1.002 The user must be able to say things such as: “Open this link.”
“Click the pricing button.” “Take a screenshot.” “Send me a screenshot
of the current page.” “Go through this website.” “Log into my account
using the credentials I provided.” “Connect my Google account.” “Connect
Base44.” “Connect Lovable.” “Open my Google Calendar.” “Create a
calendar event.” “Use my connected GitHub.” “Inspect this website.”
“Build this in Base44.” “Continue the coding task in the connected
coding environment.” “Check this website and tell me what happens.”
“Download the report.” “Upload this file.” “Fill out the form.” “Use the
authorized account.” “Complete the workflow.” “Show me exactly what the
website looks like.” “Click every relevant link and investigate.” “Use
the best available connected tool.” “Find the right MCP app
automatically.”

9.1.003 The agent must convert natural-language requests into an
executable plan when the request requires multiple actions.

9.1.004 The plan must be generated dynamically from the user’s intent,
available tools, connected integrations, permissions, current
application state, and task constraints.

9.1.005 The agent must not require the user to know which tool, MCP
server, browser action, API, model, or provider is needed.

9.1.006 The user should describe the outcome; KYMEX AI should determine
the appropriate execution mechanism.

9.1.007 If several execution mechanisms are available, the orchestration
engine must select the most appropriate one using capability,
authorization, latency, reliability, cost, data locality, and task
requirements.

9.1.008 Tool selection must be dynamic.

9.1.009 The system must not hard-code a small finite list of
applications as the only supported integrations.

9.1.010 The architecture must support discovery and registration of new
MCP-compatible applications without requiring the core agent to be
rewritten for every newly available application.

9.1.011 The system must be capable of supporting a very large and
continuously changing ecosystem of MCP servers, connectors, tools, and
applications.

9.1.012 The prompt must explicitly state that the examples of
applications listed elsewhere are examples, not the complete integration
universe.

9.1.013 The agent must be designed for thousands to tens of thousands of
potential connected applications and tools.

9.1.014 The agent must discover capabilities from provider metadata and
connection state instead of assuming capabilities from an application
name.

9.1.015 The agent must distinguish between: - an application being
known, - an application being discoverable, - an application being
connected, - an application being authorized, - a tool being
available, - a tool being permitted, - and an action actually
succeeding.

9.1.016 These states must never be conflated.

  ------------------------------------------
  9.2 UNIVERSAL TOOL / CAPABILITY REGISTRY
  ------------------------------------------

9.2.001 Create a Universal Capability Registry.

9.2.002 The registry must maintain normalized metadata for all tools
available to KYMEX AI.

9.2.003 A capability record should contain, where available: - provider
ID - application ID - MCP server ID - tool ID - tool name -
human-readable description - input schema - output schema -
authorization requirements - required scopes - risk classification -
supported environments - supported regions where relevant - latency
estimate - reliability metrics - pricing/credit characteristics - rate
limits - supported media types - supported file types - supported
actions - read/write classification - destructive-action
classification - approval requirements - authentication state -
connection state - last health check - version - compatibility
information - provenance - documentation reference

9.2.004 The registry must support capability search.

9.2.005 Capability search must be semantic, not only keyword-based.
